ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e4339	Mr Federal Minister, my question is: 115/M “How has the R&D quota in Austria developed in the light of the objective set out in the Lisbon process of increasing R&D expenditure in the European comparison?”
ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e4360	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e4376	Definitely positive, honourable Member. Since 2001, our F&E quota has exceeded the 2-percent mark and is estimated to be 2.35% this year according to the first estimates of Statistics Austria. This, ladies and gentlemen, not only achieved the intermediate target of 2% set by the Federal Government in 2003, but we are also on the right track to the next intermediate target, namely 2.5 percent in 2006. You know, we've set the target 3 percent for 2010. To this end, you will be interested in the current European comparisons: The EU of the 15 has an R&D ratio of 1.95%, the OECD average being 2.26 percent – that is the European Union plus USA, Canada, Japan, but also Mexico, Korea. So we are now at a reasonable average, but that does not mean that we should not and must not increase further. I can also tell you that the relationship between the private and public sectors has also developed well. The share of the public sector is currently around 36.6 percent, should be around 33 percent according to EU-wide standards. So that's where we're on the right track. Last, but not least: A total of €5.8 billion is currently spent on R&D in Austria.

The comparison with Germany: There, only 37.9% of mothers are with three or more children.

In fact, this Social Rights Amendment Act has provided for a number of measures to improve the Austrian population, as well as a series of measures which are technical adaptations, as they are always the case in the case of social rights changes. For example, in the BKUV we create a new self-insurance in the event of minor employment as well as an extension of the service provider's tax for minor employees and can thus better ensure the people's employment. We abandon the minimum basis of contributions and set a lower limit in comparison to the ASVG. I was able to start a very positive change yesterday with the owners of two occupational health care phones, namely the one of the Alpine Donawitz and the Sickenkasse Kindberg. The Board of Directors of this health insurance company will today decide on the merger of the two occupational health telephones. We also decide today in Parliament and can thus again have positive synergies in favour of those concerned and savings in the administrative field. In this novel, we will also simplify the collection of the service fee for the e-card by defining precisely: This remains with the service providers. It is withdrawn once a year. The health card will also be cheaper – four health certificates have cost more than €14, the service fee will be €10. I can also tell you the health card works. Contrary to all calls, the test operation ended positively at the end of May. ==References====External links== ==References== Every week up to 150,000 health cards are shipped by mail. This is also a logistical challenge. Contracting doctors must also be equipped with the appropriate infrastructure and trained in it. However, we have in the meantime – thank God! – a high acceptance not only for the insured but also in the medical profession. In the Burgenland, 80 doctors were involved in the trial. During the trial, 45 other doctors from the district sought to be equipped with the appropriate infrastructure. So the pressure of the insured to come to the doctor with the card has immediately caused a rethinking! I am very glad that we will have abolished the health certificate at the end of this year. This is going gradually, depending on the rollout of the health card, and with 1. January 2006 the Sickness History, will be past. We will all have a health card and then begin immediately with the further expansion of the health card functions, namely the pharmaceutical authorisation system up to the electronic health certificate we want by 2008/2009 at the latest. We will also close a gap with this novel, which has been shown in practice. You might remember: There was a mother in Upper Austria who worked a few days between the child care allowance and a new weekly allowance and suddenly received a much lower weekly allowance. We have come up with the fact that the basis for calculating these few days causes the mothers to have a damage. We immediately repaired this – I am very proud of it – within four weeks; and we naturally repair it retroactively. In other words, no mother should have a disadvantage, all mothers have the right, if they have come into such a situation, to request a roll-up of their weekly allowance. I would ask you to understand that this cannot be done in general, because this would mean re-rolling the cases of 300,000 child care and weekly care providers – an administrative burden that costs enormous sums. I think this is very important because it is important for our children, for our young people, to get to know businesses for their professional decision and to be adequately insured on these days, including social security law. Molterer, Dr. Cap, Scheibner, Dr. Van der Bellen, colleagues on Amendment 595/A by Dr. Alexander Van der Bellen, colleagues on a Federal Constitutional Act amending the Federal Constitutional Act (B-VG), idF of the Committee Report 998 d.B. ==References====External links== Decisions to be taken by the European Parliament and the Council on the proposal from the Commission to the Council (COM(88) 588 final) The draft law annexed to the Committee's report reads as follows: "Federal Constitutional Act amending the Federal Constitutional Act The National Council decided: The Federal Constitutional Act, BGBl. No. 1/1930, as last amended by the Federal Constitutional Act BGBl. I No. 31/2005, is amended as follows: Art. 36 para. 2 B-VG is: ‘(2) As chairman, the first-place representative of the country appointed to the Presidency, whose mandate is to be elected to that party, shall be the highest number of seats in the country day or, if several parties have the same number of seats, the highest number of voters in the last election of the country; in the case of the same claims of several parties, the lot shall decide. The Landtag may, however, decide that the Presidency should be held by another representative of the country whose mandate in the Federal Council is to this party; in any case, such a decision shall require the approval of the majority of those members of the Landday whose mandates in the Landtag fall on that party. The appointment of the Vice-President shall be governed by the Rules of Procedure of the Federal Council. The President shall have the title ‘President of the Federal Council’, and his alternate shall be entitled ‘The Vice-President of the Federal Council’. Reasons According to the type in force. 36 para. 2 B-VG serves as the chairman of the Federal Council of Representatives of the country appointed to the Presidency. By the proposed type. 36 para. 2 will be authorized to the Land Days to decide that the Presidency of another of the most powerful (or ==References====External links== 35 para. 1 B-VG) representatives of the country. This will allow a country day to respond to circumstances that make the first-place representative's leadership seem unfair. Although the member of the Federal Council concerned by such a decision on the land day is thus excluded from the Presidency, he retains his mandate. The further regulation of the decision-making requirements should be the matter of the Constitutional Law, but this should be so provided that, in any event, the adoption of a corresponding decision of the Landing Day requires the approval of the majority of the members of the Landing Days whose mandates fall within the scope of that Party. This will ensure that a decision of the Landtag cannot be taken without or against the will of the Members of that Party. In practice, therefore, the election proposals will already be submitted by the representatives of the party. The version of a decision under the proposed provision is, as a condition, only possible if more than one representative of the country in the Federal Council is left out of the party making the chairman. Furthermore, such a decision may be taken at any time within the legislative period of the land day and also during the Presidency of the country concerned. As far as this amendment is concerned, I am pleased that everyone agrees.

This group of people, who now want to quit smoking and lead a healthier way of life, should be met. What I am missing from this agreement is that those companies that have contributed illegally to chemical additives that have promoted smoking beyond the normal level are not at the same time held responsible, as is the case in the American Judicature, in order to limit the damage again. There is a clear gap between the consumer on the one hand and the industry on the other. I therefore believe that we also have to take these things into account at last, that this measure is measured by two measures. In addition, I also believe that we cannot make it so easy to simply afford the tobacco products in Austria in individual steps, without looking at what is happening beyond the border, because the tobacco smuggling is today a lucrative business. And with a little more intelligence and more harmonisation in the rise in prices together with our neighbouring countries, one would also have more in the prevention of smuggling, more than by means of an ominous implementation in Austria, with the simultaneous expectation that Hungary, Slovaks, Czechs and Slovenians will follow up on their tobacco products in half or three-quarters of a year. This half or three-quarters of a year we would have had time and thus also had a smuggling prophylaxis that could have been seen. Thirdly, for a number of times, I would like to point out that it will be the task, including the health sector, to break its head over what the 4 500 disabled people, who are leading the tobacco bars in Austria, will have in the future a basis for existence. If the objectives are achieved and tobacco consumption is reduced against zero, we will have a professional group here, which we have killed as legislators. I therefore believe that it is the Austrian Parliament's concern to ensure the existence of this group and to offer them new business and new fields of employment. First steps have been taken within the framework of the monopoly law, but they are far from sufficient. So there are further steps to be taken in this respect for this largely disabled group of entrepreneurs, because it would be irresponsible for me to see only the gain of society on the one hand and simply to leave the losers of these laws in the rain; then they have to wait for their existence or then no longer have the basis for their existence. Overall, the package is in order from a health point of view, but without accompanying measures it is incomplete.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e13931	Mr President. High house! The current legislative innovations deal with several points: Improvements in the quality of training in various health professions, facilitation of training in health professions also in part-time – something which was not permitted until now, but which is very important for many women, especially for re-enters – and also for workers in health care professions. Mrs Haidlmayr, I am convinced that with this new law we are actually improving the existing situation and that we have not been wrong. I can also explain this to you: We are faced with a situation in which there are pool solutions, i.e. in which Pool workers are left to work, but as barber workers with the corresponding lack of social security, without a guarantee of qualification, in a grey labour market which is highly unsatisfactory. With the possibility of leaving workers in the healthcare sector, including in the health field, a legal possibility has been created that is quality-secure – for the first time, there will be a professional card that makes it easy, clear to the employer, the worker or the employer and the worker transfer the qualification of the person concerned verifiable – and thus allows for the compensation of different variations in the health care sector in the legal framework. I do not allow the Bazar methods to apply, Madam Members, because the 30 percent of the draft was what the experts – those who work in this field – considered necessary and informed us. I acknowledge that I recognize the fears of the trade unions, of the professional groups and that we have then agreed on 15 percent in the discussions with the professional groups concerned. It was 20 per cent, then 15 per cent, but this is not an unusual one, Mrs Deputies! Negotiations are under way to reach a compromise. And these 15 percent were a compromise between the needs mentioned by the institutions and the fears of the permanent workers in the care professions, and I think it is a good compromise. We will observe it in the next few years and will see whether it is enough or whether we have to take other measures because of the circumstances. These 15 percent are very conscious per institution, because per unit of organization it was not at all possible. You know, in hospitals there are four- and Achter-Radels, and 15 percent of a four-wheel would be a third nurse or a 15-hour nurse. Anyway, it won't be! For the first time, there are also criminal provisions, administrative penalties if the law is not complied with. In order not to be too long, let me address the other contents of this law very briefly: We also allow for the first time – I said earlier – part-time training in care professions. It is particularly important for new entrants that this measure has not yet been made possible by law. However, we are also nominating training laws, i.e. the Health and Hospital Care Act and the MTD Act, and there are a large number of professions affected. There are also changes to these professions because EU law is necessary. The former medical-technical analysts, the MTAs, will become the biomedical analysts – I am now always just saying the female form to save time, although I would like more men to be active in these professions – the former diet assistants become dietologists and nutritionists. It concerns ergotherapists, the logopedics, the orthoptists – these are those who perform ophthalmology exercises – the physiotherapists, the former radiological-technical assistants, the RTAs become radiology technicians and midwives. In addition to the designations, we allow for the first time the training of these seven MTD professions and for midwives at specialized universities in Bakkalaureat's programmes. So far, the academies have been trained at the hospitals, and that is to say, they have been financed exclusively from the hospital budgets. It's not that easy. In addition, there have always been as many trainings as the self-need has indicated, and training has been linked to the obligation of those concerned to remain in this hospital or at least in the Federal Republic of Germany for a few years, which has greatly reduced mobility and timing a permanent lack of some professions. Now it is possible to offer courses to university holders. We have also agreed on a solution with the High School Advisory Board, which I expressly thank for its flexibility, which respects the health ministry's rights in terms of content, but also does not restrict the possibilities of the High School Advisory Council. It will now be up to the recipients, the federal states, to actually convert the academies to professional universities. The possibility to do so remains. I am also very happy that we have succeeded in anchoring child intensive care for the first time. There were them, but they were not uniformly regulated. I am very pleased that the quality assurance of child care is now an important step forward, because we all know: Children are not small adults, they have their special needs, and the needs of the children are also a very special concern to me. Thank you for your approval.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e14664	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Today, we decide to amend the Blood Security Act, which on the one hand, in agreement with an EU directive, is intended to ensure that whole blood donors remain completely unpaid and, on the other hand, to be informed from now on to each donor about the purpose of its donation. The latter clearly goes towards improved patient rights and personal protection and is of course to be supported. The great increase in security of voluntary unpaid donors is particularly due to the fact that not the financial interests are in the foreground, but for them is the idea of voluntary and unpaid commitment to our society. Especially in cases of major accidents and other special needs situations, we must be able to rely on a functioning blood supply in crisis situations. Payed blood donors, on the other hand, often put others – most often economic – reflections in the foreground and could therefore simply conceal own diseases. According to WHO, the safest blood donation is the voluntary and unpaid donation. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Everyone assumes that after an accident or surgery, sufficient and sufficient safe blood can be available. Thanks to a so-called pool of voluntary and unpaid donors, which has been built and maintained by the Red Cross in Austria since the 1950s, this works perfectly. This successful system would be put at risk by paid blood donations. Commercialization or attempts at business-making would endanger the great confidence of national blood donors with about 500,000 donors per year. Austria – and also this EU directive – aims to provide itself with blood through society. Strict protection measures and additional import arrangements are intended to protect the population from possibly uncontrollable transmissions of diseases. – In addition, it should be noted that, of course, very rare blood groups are still to be imported and allowed to be imported by another country in a few individual cases. We also insist that in the event of a call for a donor due to an acute emergency and, if necessary, an expediency is granted by the blood donation for these donors. I am delighted – just before the World Flower Donation Day, which took place on 14 April. June 2005 – that this request has found broad consensus, and thank all blood donors for their free willingness to donate and the Austrian Red Cross for the very good organization.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e15624	Madam President. High house! The present Veterinary Amendment Act 2005 provides for adjustments to Austrian veterinary law to European requirements and for the further development of investigation methods. The amendments to the Pharmacovigilance Act take account of the changes in food legislation by the decision of the Food Safety and Consumer Protection Act. The European-based prohibition on the use of hormones in food-producing animals is an important contribution to strengthening the confidence of Austrian consumers in Austrian food of animal origin. This innovation also made it clear that the use of hormones for the purpose of increasing performance in Austria is not permitted. The adjustment of the European legal basis was an essential step towards strengthening Europe's position in the WTO's proceedings against imports of meat from animals treated with hormones, thus keeping home supermarket shelves free from hormone meat. The very good animal health status of Austria, which is not a matter of course within Europe due to the free movement of goods – and we are proud of our farmers! – take into account the adjustments in Articles 3 and 4. The partial monitoring of the freedom of the Austrian cattle stocks of brucelose, bovine leukosis and IBR/IPV by means of milk examinations, taking into account the epidemiological conditions, will be possible from 2006 onwards. The increased use of the milk studies enables further diseases, which are important for food safety, to be effectively included in the monitoring strategy. For the first time since last year, an Austrian-oriented zoonosis monitoring programme and the introduction of measures to control paratuberculosis are only cited as examples. The excellent health status of domestic animal stocks not only guarantees Austrian consumers safe animal food, but also offers trade advantages to domestic animal owners on the European internal market, but also leads to an ever-increasing demand for Austrian breeding animals and embryos, as well as meat and meat products from our immediate neighbours, as well as from third countries, such as Russia or Croatia. For five years, a veterinary agreement has been negotiated with the People's Republic of China in order to also serve this future, strongly growing market in domestic animal products. The conclusion of the Veterinary Agreement with the People's Republic of China in April of this year also secures a starting advantage for domestic producers and breeders vis-à-vis European competitors on the Chinese market. Early discussions with experts from the People's Republic of China on the preparation of veterinary certificates are planned for the end of June. Worldwide, interconnected trade flows with animal and plant products always lead to the introduction of pests in areas where they would never otherwise have become domestic. Thus, the adaptation of the bee disease law was necessary in order to be able to react accordingly to the intrusion of new pests, in this case beetles or wax moths. The safeguarding of beekeeping and the health of existing stocks are the basis for a comprehensive supply of local fruits and vegetables for Austrians. I would also like to pay particular attention to Mr Maier's wish to pay particular attention to the bird flu. We are also constantly in the World Health Organization, and I am willing to report on this to the next Health Committee. As the examples of today's discussion show, a functioning veterinary system is, on the one hand, the basis for ensuring domestic food production, and on the other, only through a properly equipped and trained public veterinary service can public health be ensured through targeted epidemiological clarification of zoonotic outbreaks. I would therefore like to thank all the veterinarians who ensure that this health status of our livestock remains, but also all farmers who ensure that their animals are kept healthy and above all animal-friendly in their stables.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e16547	Madam President. Federal Minister! To the German law on noise protection: We will not agree to this bill for a number of reasons. [[Abg. Neudeck: That means, that's a good law!]] I can only tell you three reasons. The first point is that of the sound cards. We have nothing against noise cards, the question is: What will happen in reality? This law provides for various types of noise, namely railways, motorways and highways, industrial facilities, national roads, airports and trams. These six different noise sources are recorded in several – whether three, four, five or six – noise cards are not yet quite clear – and are not placed over each other, not added. This means, for example, that someone who lives in Liesing in Vienna, who is affected by all these noises, will find himself on all six cards that he is affected, but de facto – when it comes to how his noise situation is made more tolerable – will be sent in the circle. It will say to one department of the Ministry of Transport, which is responsible for the flight noise maps: Well, it's not that loud with us anyway! Go to Bartenstein! This is the industrial plant near you! – He will send him to the city of Vienna, and the city of Vienna will send him to Pröll, and Pröll will say: No, I'm just responsible for putting all of this into the Internet. – In truth, the shield is moderate and does not help citizens at all. What we are demanding here is that the noise cards be merged – technically difficult, but possible. This is what the experts in hearing in committee have told us: that this is not a technical problem in itself to bring it together. And that's what we want. Second point: thresholds. There should be a threshold of 65 decibels during the day for the noise. When I look at the reality of how to handle noise on the sides of the countries of Vienna and Lower Austria – not only the highly praised red Vienna, but also Lower Austria – in practice, especially with noise, it is there that the measures against flight noise do not begin at 65 decibels in mediation, but actually stop there! They begin there at 54 decibels with the promotion of the installation of noise protection windows, the construction of winter gardens et cetera – and at 65 decibels they are in fact only offering discharge premiums, because it is clear: It's too loud and it's not reasonable. That is, where measures are stopped in practice, where measures are being stopped, and where you start to think about measures. The third point is citizens' participation. The directive clearly states: first information for citizens and second citizen participation – effective! – to draw up action plans on every point, and also to take into account what citizens have to do with them. What you are doing here is simply informing the citizens, but not taking part in the preparation of the noise protection measures. This simply shows the far-off policy of the EIA, and therefore we will not agree to it either. With the noise protection law, we want to announce the fight against the noise of the environment, because there is no uniform legislation for noise protection in Austria so far. The new Federal Environment Protection Act brings three major innovations: For the first time, noise from road and rail transport, air transport and certain industrial and commercial installations in agglomerations is being collected across the board and the heavily burdened areas are clearly identified. The results are shown in sound cards. On this basis, action plans with noise reduction measures will be drawn up and implemented. [[Abg. The last meeting of the Committee really adopted all petitions and citizens' initiatives unanimously, whether they were aware of them or allocations. Many petitions that have been postponed in the past were also assigned to committees this time. A petition that could experience this positive fate of forwarding is the one entitled “Improving the position of disabled and central disabled persons”. Ladies and gentlemen! We all know, I think, the important establishment of a works council. We also appreciate the work of the youth confidants. We are proud that it is even possible in our country to choose people with disabilities. But I'm asking you: In addition to pride, do we also bear in ourselves the knowledge of what tasks all these groups have to carry out? Do we know what rights and obligations these were imposed on them? The number of representatives, the right to hold business meetings, the right to free time or exemption, even the updating of matters – all these are cases that are strictly regulated for works councils and youth confidants, but this is different in the case of disabled persons. I ask you: Can it be dependent on the companies whether the workforce of the individual enterprises in relation to disabled persons is treated as second-class representatives? How can it happen that we reject, on a regular basis, those whom we recognize as the weakest of our society, the protection against termination and harassment that has become an important protective shield for our works councils and youth trustees in the face of injustice and shock? I can only say: The petition is assigned to the Committee – we change this state together, we create equal rights for all and we contribute to the fact that at the end of this initiative there is real equality of opportunity and that there are no endless delays in the Committee!

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e21979	Mr President. It's about petition No. 64, in concrete terms around the knot Steinhäusl on the A 1 to the exit Altlengbach. The demands of the Signatories are the establishment of technically optimal noise protection devices. Dear ladies and gentlemen! As part of the extension of the West motorway from Vienna towards St. Christophen was examined the entire section between the node Steinhäusl and the connecting point St. Christophen. In the direction of Salzburg a continuous noise protection is planned in this area, and also on the opposite side, from Altlengbach towards Knoten Steinhäusl, studies have already been carried out. The necessary noise measurements, air evaluations and surveys were carried out and, in other words, this petition was rightly filed, significant limit values were measured. This has also led those responsible to use the best noise physicists in this area in the sense of the abrasives. Over the next few weeks, representatives of the local authorities and the initiative responsible for this petition will be invited to talk, and I am convinced that an optimal solution will be found in the interests of the locals.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e22162	Mr President. High house! Having already been very intensively discussed in the Committee today, I would like to refer to Question No. 35, brought in by Mr Kummerer, on the improvement of deaf pedagogy. It is essentially about the fact that students who are deaf can also be led to Matura in economic professions at the Institute for Hearing and Hearing Disabilities via an external office of the HBLA in Vienna. - So good, so beautiful. It is less beautiful that this regulation could only take place after petitions have been filed. There could have been signals in this direction before, without petition. But it's much more important to me: What happens to those Maturants after this period of training? Where do you find a job? As a rule, a large number of economic operators and businesses are free from the obligation to hire under the Law on the Employment of Disabled Persons. Not only businesses and economic operators, but also ministries and other institutions do so, and we must therefore also ensure that these young people also have jobs after completion of the Matura. To this end, the Educational Academy would offer itself, where access restrictions currently apply to disabled people. If the physical-psychic fitness is not given – no education as a teacher, although we need a lot of professionals who can deal with disabled students. In the current integration classes, real professionals are missing to help these young people with special needs.

It is a legitimate concern of the subcontractors that – as well as the project progress – project sections of the high-performance ÖBB line will be avoided by environmental impact assessments. It has also been clearly demonstrated that the hours these 317 000 people voluntarily provide will generate a national economic value of €56 million in Styria. However, this study has also made it quite clear that the fire brigades in Austria have to deal with the most massive financial problems. These are the following: More and more fire brigades are in the technical field, and the fire brigades are exposed to an increased risk of hepatitis through the recovery of accident victims. I hope that you will vote in favour of me and my group so that it can really be helped to break through.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-06-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00113_d7e22426	Mr President. High house! Given the current development in the Causa barracks closures, I would like to refer to three petitions aimed at maintaining individual barracks sites. It was foreseeable that, following the recommendation of the Federal Army Reform Commission to close up to 40 percent of the barracks in Austria, there would be fronts in the individual federal states that would mainly prevent the closure of the barracks. [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Since yesterday, it has been fixed that more than a third of all federal governments, specifically 26 out of a total of 71 barracks, will be closed. In Burgenland, four locations are affected, with a time horizon until 2008. A result that is not optimal, but realistic and portable under the given conditions. In this way it was taken into account that the Burgenland has a 400-km-long Schengen external border, and in future it will be ensured that at least the German army's assistance can be maintained. Nevertheless, one cannot resist a Déjà-vus. The barracks closures are, as has already been pointed out, the seamless continuation of those operations that we have seen in the closure of the post offices, the gendarmerie posts and the courts. Our fears that the closure of numerous barracks will result in a further weakening of infrastructure and rural areas could not be removed. Many barracks are a very important economic factor in rural areas. It is hardly surprising, therefore, that this foreseeable development in the affected population in the region causes helplessness, fear, anger and resentment and can be channelled in the form of corresponding petitions and citizens' initiatives. - Thank you.
